ParlaMint-IT_2015-07-30-LEG17-Senato-sed-494.ana.u9	Mr President, I would like to explain Amendment No 01,200. Before we proceed, however, I am obliged to point out that last night the President of the Senate announced that the timetable had not changed, that we are in a strange situation. I would like to raise the question again here: tomorrow morning, at 9.30 a.m., explanations of vote on this draft law are provided and, while this deadline has been given to this draft law imposing almost an urgent procedure, yesterday we heard from the voice of the representative of the Government and then in the letter itself sent by Minister Padoan to the Supervisory Committee for the renewal of positions that we are actually doing a job that will certainly not have any influence on the appointment of the new board of directors, which will instead be appointed by the law bearing his name, President protempore. Honestly, I really do not understand why we have to continue working with very tight times, when a lot of issues remain open and we could do much more work. I would now like to explain this amendment 01,200, aimed at introducing an article and dealing with the principles, that is to say, the fundamental issue to be addressed when a reform of the RAI is pomped up: I am referring to the need to re-establish, first of all, the principles on which the public service is re-arranged and reorganized. To us it seemed absolutely fundamental to try to re-examine this question, precisely in premise to a bill that is pompously titled: Reform of RAI and public service radio and television. Among other things, the reflections made at the end of the general discussion by Senator Zavoli (which, unfortunately, are reflected little in the text of this bill), lead us to say that this had to be one of the objects to investigate: in fact, when we approach the idea of reform, we need to redefine the mission of the public service. Especially at a time of ever greater transformation in the world of the mass media, giving back a mission to the public service should perhaps have been one of the fundamental issues to be addressed. We therefore believe that it is necessary to put forward the question of principles with force, establishing that access to communication is a fundamental right of freedom enshrined in our Constitution and that pluralism, freedom of access and the absence of dominant and monopoly positions must be guaranteed in the communication system and in the markets that make up it. When we are talking about the absence of dominant and monopoly positions, we are alluding to the fact that, unfortunately, the House of Deputies has long been immemorial (I am referring to the beginning of this parliamentary term), without having taken any step forward, the draft law on conflict of interest, which also this Government - President Renzi, first - had undertaken immediately to carry forward. There is another question within the framework of the principles that - I repeat - should be at the head and pre-ordered to this bill. I am referring to the redefinition of public service, which is the essential tool for achieving effective freedom of access to communication and is a real common good. If we all believe that the right to information, pluralism and freedom of access are constituent elements of the system of rights that are part of our republican pact, then it is clear that public service, which is the essential instrument for achieving this, can only be considered a real common good, which is a heritage that allows - or should allow - today ever more to guarantee the orderly, harmonious and democratic development of society. How, then, is public service characterized in this being a common good? Its mission is to promote the democratic, social and cultural development of human rights in every society and the right of every citizen to receive and disseminate information, ideas and opinions through non-discriminatory access to all available transmission platforms. Here, however, the other fundamental question emerges: public service is such because it must first preserve pluralism in the media. These communication activities carried out in the various media are carried out by the public service operator and also by private entities in accordance with the constitutional rules. The free economic activity in the media sector is and must also be exercised in accordance with the principles of pluralism and competition, established by the European Union, but safeguarding the universality of access to networks. The public service must be carried out in complete independence and editorial, administrative and financial autonomy. This principle should be the cornerstone around which to reorganize and reform the RAI governance system. The measure today to our examination and the proposal of governance that is made to us, from this point of view (independence and autonomy editorial, administrative and financial), it seems to me that they are absolutely not guarantors, because they put in fact the RAI (through the managing director and the system of the director constituted by the president and the two representatives of the Government) under the control of the Government, reducing the criterion of independence. Another fundamental issue, which should have been the subject of the measure and which, alas, will end up in the so broad delegations provided for in the draft law, is that the public service must, in order to be such, ensure adequate levels of access to all the different technological opportunities, independent information and communication products and must ensure this to all citizens, regardless of the social conditions and the spending capacity of each citizen. The seventh element that we set out is the mission to promote culture, knowledge, national productions and to ensure all the appropriate ways of achieving new forms of communication by citizens, because this is also one of the tasks of the public service today. We are very sorry that all these amendments (there are also other, different ones concerning the basic principles) have been completely neglected both in the debate and in the very nature and articulation of the draft law, so much so that many points are in total contrast with these principles, which come directly from Article 21 of the Constitution.
ParlaMint-IT_2015-07-30-LEG17-Senato-sed-494.ana.u10	Mr President, I am beginning to talk to you about the amendments tabled to Article 1, which concern the mission of the RAI, i.e. those defining general principles, which should come before any debate on governance. In general discussion we had started from there, because it is the most common sense thing. Faced with a public service vetusto, technologically backward and with a company composition inadequate to the technological challenges of renewal that it has to face (as it is before everyone's eyes), we must define as the first thing that public service will have to be configured and what its main tasks will be. The RAI teacher of the Italians of the last century has objectively changed: the RAI has changed, the Italian social fabric has changed, the market has changed, the technological environment in which the citizen lives has changed; the RAI should therefore aim for a complete revolution because the generalist television, as we know it, has short life, will last still very little. Yesterday I spoke on the order of business on what Mrs De Petris mentioned a moment ago, namely that we are now voting on a board of directors, perhaps next week as we understand it, in order to keep it three years with a law against which everyone, beginning with the President of the Council, had launched stroli, which represents the partitioning of RAI, the division on the basis of political interests, clientele and party and not of efficiency, performance capacity and independence, above all, of the public information service. Well, we said just that: if we spend the next three to five years with a board of directors and with a certain governance, because on it then depend on the nominations of the directors of network, of the directors of news headlines and therefore precisely of those who make the television, that is that they direct the apartments that make the television physically and make strategic choices for the company, and we take people who do not have the appropriate skills, we will not be able to make the revolution necessary to RAI, also because we are now late. Today we are talking about Netflix and technologies that use platforms completely different from those used to RAI. I have repeatedly mentioned, during the hearings in the RAI Supervisory Committee, the fact that RAI has no clear strategic vision on web policy. We find ourselves with public television and websites that have classifications, compared to the reference tables, ridiculous. For example, our news channel, RaiNews, is over two hundredth in Italy despite the investments that have been made. I remember that only last year, for the renewal of RaiNews had been paid 80 million euros. Of course there has been a technological revival, but then in the end the share stops at 0.3%. Given that RAI does not have to live on share, as we all know, we have a schizophrenic structure in which some areas grow in a disproportionate way, absorb resources and produce little in comparison perhaps with a harmonisation between internal and external competences, between a balance relative to what RAI must do, the mission that it must have and therefore the tools of which it must equip itself, absolutely doing so. The first question, therefore, is to ensure that RAI becomes a real public service like those of other countries that we have often heard quoted. It is clear that if we do not first think about what to do we cannot think after what governance we can use to achieve those objectives and what competences the members of the board must have to achieve them. At that point we can talk about the canon and how much resources are needed to manage the indicated mission. Undoubtedly the RAI has the virtues, virtuous situations and great professionalism within it but it also has a set of devices now elephantic and disproportionate and an accumulation of managerial offices that, over the years, have taken away resources. When we talk about politics that intervenes in the public service and the parties from which RAI must be saved, we talk about this, we talk about mediocrity. It happens, in fact, that in order to have an internal referent I make a career for a less competent person, because it is clear that a very good professional will not put his face on failed projects, losers or that they do not have a real purpose like a good news, a good production chain of entertainment, documentaries and anything else. This whole set of amendments to Article 1 therefore defines a number of suggestions, which are already contained in the public service contract, but which is probably right to discuss in this House too, depending on what we are going to vote on in Article 2, namely the true form of governance, dealing in particular with the proposals that we have made and which have been partly transposed and partly not, but which we will talk about during the discussion of the amendments to that article, in order to define a competence, which allows us to ferry the RAI from the past to the present, if only with a view to a future perspective. The first key point is to clarify the main aspects of the RAI mission, highlighting some of them, such as the media company function that RAI will have to acquire. What does this definition mean, which is often repeated in the Commissions and in this House? It involves the transformation of a Broadcaster, that is, a society that makes television in a canonical way, in a society that produces content, that have different characteristics and different purposes of sharing and use by citizens. I am referring therefore to the dissemination of content on certain platforms, such as mobile phones or the web, in the face of a society which, as you know, is fundamentally backward from this point of view. The majority of RAI spectators are older than 55 or 60 years of age and are often people with low computer literacy. It would therefore be essential for RAI itself to carry out an information operation on the new media, aimed at citizens who are not yet so experienced and so ready to use and enjoy the content of information, entertainment and culture on other platforms, thus starting to do a real didactic activity on the use of new technologies. Perhaps this has not yet been sufficiently stressed by everyone, even though we have received a number of suggestions in the Commission for the general direction and supervision of broadcasting services during a number of hearings. [[Call the president back.]] I shall conclude, Mr President, by analyzing the amendment by amendment. I therefore believe that computer literacy is one of the main points that RAI will have to address, together with a clear, precise and multi-platform plan to relaunch the public information service. We look like another planet than the government and the majority intend to reform in order to this important law. ParlaMint-IT_2015-07-30-LEG17-Senato-sed-494.ana.u13	Mr President, a measure which has its own logic, its own meaning, starts with a programme, an objective and then is consistent with it. The slogan with which the Government presented this bill was "outside the parties from the RAI", an impeccable slogan - I believe that in the bibliography that concerns this company, from the first to the second Republic has always been mentioned - but that has never been implemented. Well, I think that exactly the same thing is being done on this occasion, because in reality there is only a shift in governance from an influence, from a privileged relationship with Parliament, to a relationship in which the role of the Government is certainly more important, in some respects heavier. This scheme has led me to table amendments, which are partly a provocation. I start from a premise: if indeed - and we must be consistent, putting aside all hypocrisy - the objective is to take away the parties from the RAI, there is only one road, the main road, and it is exactly that of the privatization of the company, a privatization that can also be done; it has been talked about and has been authorized also in the Government Letta by a representative of government like Catralà. In other words, it is possible to create the conditions by which the public service is in fact the service and the public entity becomes private, i.e. RAI. At that point, the public service becomes a sort of task, of service precisely, that the Government, the State can put into competition with a contract, according to certain criteria and creating certain requirements that must be met, in which all private networks and RAI participate. Such an operation would completely take the policy out of management and at the same time give the opportunity to express an opinion on these service pieces that are carried out by private networks. In this way that problem, that kind of slogan, that objective underlying the reform in fact would be achieved. Why do I say it's a provocation? In fact we start from the idea of pulling the parties out of RAI, but then we only try - I use an expression used very much in these years - to occupy it. What is happening with this kind of reform, which in fact only concerns governance, is precisely this because we create a condition for the government to become the dominus in its relationship with the public service. It is clear that such a thing, at least to me, cannot be well, starting from another assumption. I am not opposed to the idea of going ahead with the appointment of the new summit through the Gasparri law because, apart from the fact that we have legal obligations, it is evident above all that we cannot leave a company that manages three billion public money with a summit that should be continuously extended, but not only until September: taking into account the times of approval of this bill, we would practically arrive at the end of October. If we consider that within the law it is also provided that the new board of directors must even decide how to elect a representative of the workers, who would be on the new board of directors, we will arrive in November. I believe that this is impossible and I think that this should be a common opinion. However, it could give us a great chance: which one? It is clear that all the haste in dealing with this issue was determined by the expiry RAI summit. If we solve the problem in this way, this impellence that, above all, makes us deal with the issue only from the point of view of governance, would fail. If we renew the summit with the old law it would be much more logical to give more space to the debate. Let's face the truth: we only deal with governance in a system that, on the other hand, is changing and needs other interventions. In fact, the draft law provides for delegations to the Government on important issues that could be addressed in Parliament. In my opinion there is a contradiction between the type of choice made by the Government, which I share (i.e. renewing the summit through the Gasparri Act), and the choice, not to take the most positive aspect of it, namely the enlargement of the spaces for a much more in-depth discussion, which could lead to a more common position. Unfortunately, this has not been done. I believe that, from this point of view, we risk moving forward with a confrontation that will not reform RAI and that risks only giving the idea, for the umpteenth time, of an impotence on the part of Parliament and the forces of politics to reform the largest cultural industry in the country. Above all, we continue to move forward with patterns that are always the same. In the end, the essence of this measure is all about governance and about self-management, creating a thousand contradictions. As I have already said in general debate, it is clear that it is difficult to pass as a historical change and a liberation of RAI from the snares of politics from a law which in any case provides for the involvement of Parliament to another in which the role of the Government is predominant. It is a great hypocrisy that takes us nowhere. In my opinion, we should have the courage and the ability to say, at such a time, since we are in August, that we can appoint the new summit and reopen a wider debate, by including in the debate issues which, on the other hand, are now merely referred to, in fact, the decisions of the Government, also creating a grey area, in which it clearly becomes difficult to understand what is the central reason for the debate.

If this criterion had been applied to RAI - starting from the foundations and not only from governance, as we do when we want to do things right in order to build buildings that can last and be recognized over time - we would not have come to pursue only a small and modest objective, for which however this has not been useful, because the cda will not be elected with the criterion that we are approving. You look like Alice's rabbit in wonderland, who is always in a hurry, but doesn't know; not only doesn't have a mission, but doesn't even have a vision, a minimum objective. I do not want to disturb rhetorical terms, but if the objective is to renew it with other criteria, which we do not agree on, it no longer serves the purpose: in the next few hours - days at most, as we are told in the Rai Supervisory Committee - we will proceed with the same method. There have also been very small openings in the Commission on some aspects: we have said that the fee becomes a tax of purpose and that, at the same time, there is proportionality with respect to income, and many other things that are the subject of delegation to the Government (as you know, once again with the motivation that we need to proceed to the pace of battle). We have an opportunity: the mistake you have made, there is no doubt, and I hope that, instead of relying on various communications, authoritative or not, it is said serenely that the embarrassment is obvious. It will therefore be necessary to convey knowledge to the people who follow the public service towards what it should be or that it will certainly be the world of the future, when we will also transform the industry and therefore when all or a large part will travel by cable, fiber optics and also with the system of convergence, of which RAI is part through its subsidiary Rai Way, of everything that concerns mobility, that is, mobile phones and connections. Remember that we talked about the fact that the 700 megahertz band, on which Rai Way operates, will be used for mobile phones and this interface with all the tools. Coming to the RAI mission, we need to make sure more and more that people are able to have important programs. It comes to mind when in a RAI program of many years ago "Back all" was mocking RAI and it was said that "life is a quiz." I wish it had been Eduardo's classic theater, which RAI broadcast many years ago. We could say a thousand things, talk about culture, about the strength that the country has, we could imagine a news that is no longer made with sandwiches (we know well what the sandwich is), we could try to give people what is important and if we do not really know what the mission of RAI is, how can we imagine that members of the board of directors, who should then implement that mission, besides of course everything that is foreseen in the contract of service, can implement it well, if they do not have profoundly clear what this mission is? We are therefore debating this Article 1, there are many amendments which represent a whole series of premises, and then there are other additional amendments which we will discuss later, in which we are talking about AGCOM, the Control Authority on which there is a speech to be made later. We should assume an RAI that has a great strength and the daring to think of itself as an instrument that is not only of communication, but of interface with people, that makes people recover the desire to feel part of a project that is the Nation. As long as the RAI is of the State, this could and should do more usefully: to make citizens feel involved in the overall transformation project of a nation, in which everyone feels deeply involved in what they have to do together to try to imagine a different country and this is perhaps what we should really do, beyond the Sunday entertainment broadcasts that serve nothing but to make the spectators sane from the mental point of view. We should make the mind a little dry, because that dryness puts you in a position to be critical. Perhaps this is the real mission of a public service and we will have to work to ensure that this is what the new board of directors feels within itself and succeeds in transmitting through the action of RAI, which must stop being just an instrument of inebetting of the people who are there watching television. There are people, as well as my mother, who say that television has said it; we are still at that point; instead we have to start wondering if what television says is right and if you can interact with that device to make it stimulate the questions. Perhaps the real mission of RAI should be to stimulate questions, then each one finds the answers he wants. If RAI stimulates questions to people, we would do our job well.
ParlaMint-IT_2015-07-30-LEG17-Senato-sed-494.ana.u16	Mr President, let us look at the reform of RAI as a public service. The colleague asked what is the mission of a public service such as RAI. It is certainly information, but RAI played a key role at the beginning of its operational mandate because it actually played a cultural and educational role. He taught Italian to all the people because everyone spoke with his dialect at the time and, therefore, had a fundamental and priority role in the diffusion of culture. Now, luckily, times have changed and the media have changed too. There are many more: the transversality and immediacy of the IT media, social media, the Internet and everything else make sure that everything about information and culture is managed differently. In any case, for our culture, we are of the idea that public service and large transmissive networks - from transport to energy, but also information and the Internet - must be state-owned and managed by the State. The problem is that, when we talk about state management, politics manages by manipulating everything. Policy management becomes interference. And that's not what it should be. The management of a public service by the State should be the safeguarding of the rights and duties of this service. A managed service is made ad hoc so that it fits around the citizenship and the need of a citizen to be informed. Now we certainly have other media that are more immediate and capillaries: each of us always has a mobile phone or a computer access. It is true that it does not affect all age groups or citizenship, but we have a fairly widespread spread and, therefore, you might wonder if it makes sense. The Internet is an extreme source of innumerable information, but often you fall into fallacious information. Therefore, the user and the user who goes to receive the information must still have a critical conscience, which is not innate but must form. Therefore, it must have access to information in a cross-cutting and objective manner. If, at the time of access to the information, these are already manipulated a priori, an objective opinion cannot be obtained. It is therefore essential to have at least one source that can be objectively defined as a neutral source. We can be quite utopian because anyone who has an opinion influences even the exposure of facts. However, the assumptions dictated by these missions for the RAI are certainly not a good hope because we have always had the interference of the parties within the RAI. The government proclamations said to take away the parties. It has already been said by colleagues that we remove part of the interference of the parties to include those of the Government. This is certainly not a good thing, because, at the time when information is being transmitted by the Government, it is not possible to have the objectivity that, instead, should be inherent in the information itself. This is certainly very restrictive - as Mr Cioffi said - because it involves the lack of a critical conscience in the citizens, and a capacity to ask questions. The questions are fundamental: when a person asks himself questions, it is because he wants cultural growth and to go under the crust of things in order to be able to grasp the true hidden meaning. Starting from the assumption that the large infrastructure must be state-owned - here we are talking about Rai Way, which is on sale - but it is absolutely necessary to maintain autonomy. The State must, therefore, intervene to ensure that information autonomy is guaranteed. Even yesterday we had signs that the government interferes, interferes and accelerates the times, ensuring that parliamentary work, which is not neutral but at least more plural, is affected even by a letter that implies the taking of action that must go on anyway, even if we have other issues and priorities. I understand that the Management Board has expired and needs to be renewed. But all the work done in the Commission has been aimed at mitigating certain interferences and improving, as far as possible, the implementation and structure of the Management Board, also with a view to improving its professional capacity and ensuring that the information is more attractive and the television system more performing. If we abandon this road - I remember that already now the information ship does not navigate in the best waters - and let ourselves be influenced by a letter from the Government, starting immediately with a wild lottery and with old rules, we certainly do not do a good service to the State. I still hope that the government will not take a step back, but at least can review its position in order to improve the method of election of the board of directors, avoiding the exercise of a political influence towards it. [[Applause from the M5S Group.]]

I therefore believe that talking about theatre (but also lyrics, dance and prose) in a law is very important. Ours is a country that has had great playwrights and great actors and perhaps we should return to valorizing the theater in an appropriate way. This could allow us to return to what we were talking about before, that is, to an RAI that also carries out a cultural service, that can increasingly increase people's critical abilities; theatre can be a great tool in this sense and therefore I consider it important to quote it in a law. I therefore believe that Senator Crosio's amendment is correct. Indeed, I support it and declare my vote in favour.

ParlaMint-IT_2015-07-30-LEG17-Senato-sed-494.ana.u98	I ask you to speak for an explanation of vote.

ParlaMint-IT_2015-07-30-LEG17-Senato-sed-494.ana.u100	FUCKSIA (M5S). Mr President, I would ask you to accept Amendment No 1,392, which comes after some amendments that talk about everything and the opposite of everything, even with surprise. I saw the interest of the members of the League in the previous amendments, which is new, in a whole series of aspects; there was no specific reference to immigrants, but they were somehow highlighted because we talked not only about folklore and tourism, but also about respect for different ethnic cultures. So this seemed new to me, but there was everything and the opposite of everything. Even there was the incentive for non-profit organizations performing shows on the territory. So, in the end, in the previous amending proposals there was the protection of Made in Italy, but it was not understood in what form. While I fully support this amendment. We are talking about a very important issue: the protection of safety at work, which is very dear to me, which is often improperly spoken of, perhaps more because of electoral consensus or in the wake of accidents and fatal accidents that fill the front pages of our newspapers. You always talk to people's belly. Safety, on the other hand, is something that must enter everyday life. We must make a culture of security and learn to distinguish what is useful from what is merely a bureaucratic formality. I have had experiences in the past of films shown in schools (projects with ANMIL), in which they showed the boys, especially the boys of technical institutes, the episodes in which they told the dynamics of an accident, of how an untrained apprentice, maybe salaried in black, went to work in a company and distractedly put his hand under a press and suffered a serious injury. Well an image of that kind is so incisive, it remains so in memory and is so formative, more than a thousand pages written, because the strength of images and the strength of witness give a teaching that so many abstract words cannot transmit. We, with the implementing decrees of the jobs act, had the opportunity to make the changes simpler and more effective also with regard to safety. But this has not been understood, nor by us, nor by the Government, because there is no preparation on this subject. We are going to legislate, it is a technical problem, but we often do not know how. Then we continue to take action, even if fatal accidents have dropped (for the number of hours worked less, actually). But we do have situations that leave us very disconcerted. Recently we had, for example, the fire of Fiumicino and the even more recent fire of Modugno. Then one does not explain why, despite the abundant legislation in force for so many years, an all too detailed rule then is not precautionary even for the occurrence of these accidents. So the hope is that there will be broadcasts that talk about this topic and that they will call on people who are competent to do so, from the technicians of the prevention of ASL to those who tell us a bit about the history of medicine and safety in the workplace. We are the home of Ramazzini, Devoto, the first institute of occupational medicine in Milan. On the other hand, we often put ourselves on the defensive side of European directives and legislation, when we have absolutely nothing to learn. I remember that, when the machinery directive came out, we practically did a size and glued what we already had into the machinery directive; everything had already been transposed. Then, to return to making programmes on these issues means first of all to raise awareness of the importance of safety and to work safely. It also means pointing out to the worker that work is a duty, right and value and is all the more valuable as it is the gratification and expression of human abilities and the realization of the human being. That is why I call for this amendment to be signed on behalf of the 5 Star Movement Group. The 5 Star Movement, in fact, is in favour of the protection of all, the protection of the last, it is in favour of giving right to all, even if it is not absolutely in favour of assistantism, as is often reproached in the name of a right because, in reality, it is well aware that before everything should be tried to give a job to everyone. Work must be seen as a right and the State should engage in this. But work as a right also means safe work, therefore right to realization and right not to be exposed to risks and dangers. Recently, however, this Government, which fills its mouth so much and gets beautiful on certain topics in the conferences or when the victims claim attention, has made available a decree relating to failures and ILVA in which practically a company is authorized to profit and move forward without the protection measures. I urge you to think about this. What will happen if one day, working under conditions against the law and against current prescriptions, among other things generic, then another accident happens? Therefore, let us make a culture of security, teach it and make it a daily act, and let us all subscribe to this amendment. [[Applause from the M5S Group.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2015-07-30-LEG17-Senato-sed-494.ana.u128	STEFANI (LN-Aut). Mr President, I rise to point out to the House the content of this amendment which we will of course be voting in favour of. Since the radio and television service provided by RAI is a public service and everyone is required to pay the fee, which is not in proportion to the service offered but only in relation to the ownership of the instrument, we are particularly interested in the fact that this service offers a certain type of programming. In point (a) of amendment 1,418 we are talking about information programmes and general deepening also with regard to institutional and parliamentary information. In fact, we mean that the service provided must not be an information service - pass me the term - governmental, but must be based as much as possible on a criterion of pluralism, which is not only the paredicium in the electoral campaign. In fact, when information is given through the TGs and the various services, the possibility of representing, with reference to each individual problem, not only the topic supported by the government majority, but also, in particular, the point of view of the various forms of opposition must be ensured. It is also very important - even if I do not read it all - that is contained in point (c), which deals with cultural promotion programmes and headings. Also in this case it is very important to value all those broadcasts and documentaries that have as their objective the promotion and the valorization of tourism and made in Italy in the world. Being a period of crisis and considering that the same sector for many years has been a carrier also in Northern Italy, where the manufacturing sector is suffering a strong decline, we must think that Italy was born for tourism and has all the resources to be really a pole of attraction for the whole world. Equally important in point (f) is the promotion of programmes for children in the original language. It seems a banality, but the mind of a child has a remarkable receptivity and if you transmit programs of this type you will also have the possibility of an education to learn foreign languages. ParlaMint-IT_2015-07-30-LEG17-Senato-sed-494.ana.u191	CHAMPOLILLO (M5S). Madam President, ladies and gentlemen, the purpose of this agenda is to allow for the clear transmission of the two channels of the Chamber and Senate on digital terrestrial channels today, unfortunately, only visible on the satellite platform and therefore visible only by those who own a satellite TV decoder for payment or by the very few owners of so-called free-to-air satellite decoders. I do not think that the government and the current majority are missing the importance of ensuring that every citizen is able to watch Parliament's sessions free of charge on television. The direct knowledge of what is happening in Parliament, of how a process of formation and discussion of a specific legislative provision takes place, the behaviour of political forces and individual parliamentarians are, in my opinion, issues of such constitutional importance that they cannot be entrusted only to free-to-air or paid satellite television and therefore, as is often the case in Italy, guaranteed exclusively to those who have the economic means to benefit from satellite television reception. Access to the television vision of parliamentary work is a right of every citizen and must be guaranteed equally to everyone. Moreover, the potential and the diffusion of digital terrestrial also allow the technical to be able to easily realize all this. The RAI has five mux for a bandwidth capacity of over 100 megabits so it is technically possible to insert two national LCNs in clear on digital terrestrial. The question, therefore, is why it has not been considered, until now, to make a specific commitment to this end. The doubt, in other words, is that such non-commitment by public institutions and RAI finds its reason in fear of being able to have a citizenship that expresses itself freely and without the filter of a journalistic chronicle very often, as we have seen, partial and biased. There is much talk of transparency of the institutions and openness of the palaces of power to the citizens: therefore, the free and clear vision of parliamentary work through the implementation of two national channels, the so-called LCN, would certainly be a significant signal in this direction. The 5 Star Movement is on the side of the citizens and continues to feel on every single parliamentary initiative, like the one we are talking about, that only by putting oneself at the service of the citizens can we give a true meaning to what we do here, every day, in Parliament. That is why I call for this agenda to be voted on and approved by everyone. [[Applause from the M5S Group.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2015-07-30-LEG17-Senato-sed-494.ana.u206	Madam President, with regard to the amendments we have tabled to Article 3, the most important - we can also revise it in the explanation of vote - is the exclusion of invitations or invitations to tender from television products, transmission time and so on. This is because, even with an absolutely transparent procedure, RAI is obliged to buy projects or products - for example, a documentary - made by companies that then realize them too. Then, to acquire a project and then to race to give it to someone else would certainly create an inappropriate product: it is not the usual practice that exists in this economic and productive sector, so this is the main point. Then there is another amendment that concerns the exclusion of the managing director from the roofs provided for other members of the board of directors, employees, non-artistic advisors of RAI. The RAI, as we have had the opportunity to say on several occasions, has to face a remarkable revolution: it is still a failed structure, from a certain point of view, because it is too large, because it is employed in too many sectors. It therefore needs profound rationalization. To do this, even in the light of the CEO profile that the Government proposes in this bill, with great, immense decision-making powers, we need a real professional. This - I repeat - compared to the mediocrity that has caused the interference of politics, parties, client interests within the RAI, we really need to change mentality, change strategy and think instead of finding a good competent manager in the areas that concern RAI's action, therefore in the editorial field (a word that is little used talking about RAI). RAI, in fact, is the largest Italian cultural industry - it is said - but it is also a great publishing company and it takes someone competent in that field, also in the field of market, in the sector where RAI moves: purchase of products, sales. Demanding all this competence and this high performance from the managing director, we cannot then claim the profile of one that earns 200,000 euros; unfortunately, in fact, in today's market these are not the prices. It is therefore appropriate, in the consistency of the request for a very high competence, not to set such a restrictive ceiling solely for the figure of the managing director. Madam President, I shall shortly pass on a list of amendments which I intend to withdraw. PRESIDENT. - The debate is closed. Thank you, Senator. If you pass it on to the Presidency, it will simplify our work.
ParlaMint-IT_2015-07-30-LEG17-Senato-sed-494.ana.u207	Madam President, we have proposed a number of amendments to Article 3. Senator Airola rightly recalled certain specifications relating to the Procurement Code, which we discussed at the delegation law already approved by this branch of Parliament and under discussion in the Chamber of Deputies. Paragraph 2 of Article 3 refers to the fact that works, services and supplies below the thresholds of Community importance are not subject to procedural obligations and so on. We would like to recall what we said during the debate on the contract code: we are talking about substantial amounts, because the threshold of Community importance for works is EUR 5.150,000 and, for services and supplies, it is about EUR 200,000. This provision, therefore, is partly contrary to what we have said in relation to the contract code (but we will see these things as we discuss the amendments). There are, then, a few other aspects, which we will discuss in detail when we arrive at the specific amendment. For example, with Amendment 3,218 we explain that the CEO must make everything transparent, while respecting, of course, the protection of personal data, in relation to the tasks entrusted: everything must be readable, so that there is a great capacity for transparency of the RAI, which we think is really important. We had also provided for a provision concerning the determination of the Authority for Energy and Gas, which specifies that consultancy tasks cannot be paid unless the publication of the acts with which the assignment was entrusted, the compensations and other things of this kind is made known in an absolutely transparent manner. On the other hand, all we do is make the provisions of an important Authority such as the Authority for Energy and Gas. Then there are other aspects that we will see specifically when we talk about the amendments in detail. Perhaps we should intervene point by point, so that we can be clearer in the vote. In fact, apart from the general explanation of the amendments relating to a whole article, it is good that every time we vote on specific amendments, we take action to make the House better educated on what we are voting on.
ParlaMint-IT_2015-07-30-LEG17-Senato-sed-494.ana.u208	Madam President, on Article 3 we have tabled, in particular, two amendments, on 3.1 and 3.8, which I shall briefly present. Amendment 3.1 is deleted from Article 3, because we believe, in essence, that the powers and functions of the current Director-General should be confirmed. In this article 3 we talk about the CEO and, therefore, his responsibilities and his powers. We will speak on the specific amendments, because the matter will come back, from here to the end of the debate. We believe that this Managing Director appointed by the Government who, even if with a filter operated by the Board of Directors, is substantially appointed by the Government, has excessive powers and goes to unbalance precisely that harmony of relations guaranteed by the law in force. Among other things, this law will be applied for the fourth time in the coming days. And it's a record for that law, which is having more applications than critical. There have been many criticisms, but there are countless applications. In that current law, which currently regulates the production of the public service, the Director-General is appointed by the Government, the shareholder, the Treasury. We know that perfectly well. But this new text of law replaces the Director General a CEO because it is said that, in the end, RAI is a company like all the others. But the public service, which was discussed this morning (and there were also comments from Senator Mineo, who recalled some definitions that were not accepted) has its own specificity. Not just because the State, the Treasury, owns 99% of the shares. The remaining symbolic share has the Siae, a particular that few know. But the Siae, in fact, is a public reality in that it is the society of authors and publishers that must protect copyright, and it is a symbolic presence, while the Treasury has 99 percent. And then there are the famous sentences that, over 40 years, have marked the life of RAI. We believe that the article should be repealed, but then we will also come back to this in the other articles, because an imbalance is being achieved. There are specific definitions, with regard to the text of the article, that we believe should be revised. And this is a further reason for reflection for the government. We therefore criticize some of the points in this text which we are debating and voting to future memory because, in the meantime, we will apply the current law in the next few days. Then we will see, when we vote on it, when we will close the debate and when the House will deal with it. It seems to me that it is difficult for the House to continue its work in order to vote on a law which will then apply in the future. The points we have criticized are this role of the CEO in relation to the Council, which must remain Parliament's issue. Even this text, when and if it is adopted, will maintain a parliamentary facility, even if it reduces the members from nine to seven and those elected by Parliament are four out of seven. On the other hand, the current law, which will be applied, means that there are seven out of nine members elected by Parliament, even though through the Supervisory Committee. Of the other two nominated by the Government, one, who is the President, must obtain two thirds of the votes of the Supervisory Board. Therefore, if there is no transparent convergence of consensus of a significant number of members of the Supervisory Board, this President appointed by the Government does not become such. In practice, even one of the two Councillors appointed by the Government, in fact, according to the law that we approved in 2004, is of parliamentary origin, because if he does not obtain two thirds of the votes of the Supervisory Board he cannot be elected. So Parliament has the last word. Our amendments to this Article 3 focus on the imbalance between the function of the CEO and the Board of Directors. And I repeat once again that the Council is not a division, but an expression of democracy and of Parliament. If the public service is to be parliamentaryized - a term that uses the judgments of the Court, not me - it means that it must be subject to democratic control by Parliament. Then, if there is the degeneration of lotteries and partitions, it must be fought regardless, but that there is a body of summit, leadership, direction and management, like the board of directors expressed by Parliament, is an expression of democracy. The citizens express Parliament and this gives confidence to the governments, in electing the Presidents of the Chamber and Senate, the members of the Supreme Council of the judiciary and the Constitutional Court. I also say this to my colleagues who sometimes point the finger at division: let them remember that Parliament is the expression of democracy and it is clear that we are divided here (the voters voted for various lists and proposals, each of which represents a segment of the country). Is this democracy or is it division? Degenerations, then, are another question: whether you move from parties to the partycracy or whether you move from pluralism to lottification. A parliamentary source, however, is crucial. We will therefore also insist on the discussion on Article 3, as we will do on other issues, on this aspect of the relationship between Director-General/CEO and Council, as well as on the delegations, which, however, are not in Article 3, which, if granted indefinitely and too widely to the Government, would create a criticality that cannot be overcome. I also say this to my fellow Members who ask when they vote, when they debate and when they finish the examination of the draft law in question: it depends on the balance that is being held, because the amendments are not pretentious, but they highlight some issues. For us, the issue of the CEO, his powers and the delegation, of which we will discuss later, are central and resolvable de iure condendoing. Then there will be a review of the House and the law is important, but it has become no more urgent, since in the meantime it applies what there is: a bit of reflection and rationality I therefore believe that they serve everyone. The issues are essential: we have spent time on pluralism, local information and languages, forgetting, ladies and gentlemen, that RAI today has 13 channels, so now, at 1.30 p.m., it can transmit information, sport, programmes in English and those on local cultures at the same time. Sometimes we forget that the modernisation made with the previous step has created a potential. If it is then to be wondered if people will see more of the program on the cultures of the inland areas of Sardinia or Fiorello, let the viewer and the citizen decide: as I said days ago, we cannot propose in prime time on a single channel a pure praiseworthy monograph on Majakovsky. There are 13 channels, in which they will also find programs on local cultures or dedicated to minors (there are even two of the RAI, if I'm not mistaken, divided by age groups, for the youngest and for the older ones). When preparing agendas and amendments, therefore, first use the remote control at home and see that the world has already changed. Many, in fact, propose things because they think that there is only one television channel, because they do not see it: seeing it, however, serves to understand how the world has already changed.

ParlaMint-IT_2015-07-30-LEG17-Senato-sed-494.ana.u236	D'ALI' (FI-PdL XVII). Mr President, we have also had a debate on this amendment in the 5th Commission. It is truly unique the claim to keep RAI as a public company, however, freeing it from the obligations of other public companies, according to some conveniences. In this case, RAI is to be deprived of the obligation to celebrate public evidence competitions for the marketing of its productions and to allow private negotiation or direct sale to other broadcasters or to other distribution circuits. I believe that if the Government, this majority and the House reaffirm the publicistic nature of the RAI, it must be applied in everything. You can not have - I apologize the expression - the full barrel and the drunken wife: either you are a public company or you are a private company. If we privatise the RAI, the RAI will have the opportunity to sell its products by private negotiation, direct contract or as best it believes, being a private company like all the others. But if RAI produces under a public company regime, among other things also taking advantage of the contribution of citizens through the payment of the fee, then it must be subjected, also for the sale and marketing - as the text of the speakers says - of its products also artistic, to the rules that every other public company follows celebrating normal competitions. On the other hand, I do not understand why this should not happen. I see with danger the possibility of evading the procedures of public evidence in the marketing of products, especially they are mainly sold abroad, not being broadcast on the national territory by RAI, Italian television. I would not like to go over issues that have affected the news in recent years for other companies. But this possibility lends itself, according to the majority and some parts of the judiciary, also to the possibility of creating black funds - I call them that - because they are not regulated by a public evidence contest. Therefore, I would like to invite the rapporteurs, but above all the majority, to reflect: we cannot allow derogations, if RAI is a public company, to some of its activities with regard to compliance with the evidence procedures. I repeat that, if the RAI is to be fully privatised, it will be privatised, as a referendum voted by the Italian people had indicated a few years ago and that it has been adequately disregarded. Unfortunately, when it suits, they apply, when it is not convenient to neglect.

ParlaMint-IT_2015-07-30-LEG17-Senato-sed-494.ana.u252	Mr President, I hope that there may still be a conversation in this House with the Government and the rapporteurs. We find the issue of delegation to both Article 4 and Article 5 and it is extremely sensitive, in the sense that the real and real object of the draft law is the reform not of RAI but of its governance, as we will see more precisely in Article 2. Two articles, 4 and 5, which contain both delegations, were added to the text in some way. The delegation on Article 4 essentially concerns the reform of the canon and on this point we have, also in the Commission, stressed in some way the excess represented by the inclusion of this topic in the reform of governance. That there is a need for a modification of the current system, even because of the high level of evasion, seems to me a shared and consolidated fact. But it could be quietly made subject to another provision or inserted within another more punctual, and not in a specific delegation law to the Government. That is the perplexity that we have and which has led me to be the first signatory to this amendment, which is deleted from the whole article. This is an issue that I also put to the attention of the Government, taking into account - and I conclude - an aspect that we have already emphasized during other delegated laws. In our view, at least in the initial drafting - although I agree with the Commission's work on improving the text - there is an excess of generality. Here we do not identify an alternative or replacement system of the current, but it is a delegation that leaves much room for interpretation. From this point of view, we would have preferred a more precise measure in the form of a decree-law or specific amendment on another measure.
ParlaMint-IT_2015-07-30-LEG17-Senato-sed-494.ana.u253	Mr President, that of delegations is one of the central issues of this debate. We have raised the problem of the relationship between the CEO and the Council, on which some changes have been made in the Commission, while we will discuss the others later. This article contains the delegation on the canon. Of course, we believe that the canon, although unpopular and criticized, has its own logic in supporting the public service. And we think that there is also a balance of system. I say this because some people think that the fee, which finances the RAI at 50% and which allows for less commercial crowding of RAI, can serve to safeguard the aspirations or market spaces of commercial television. In fact, the biggest supporters of the canon in the information system are the newspapers, which then have various orientations. I am not saying that many people are more oriented than others, but I could also do so. Therefore, the fee also has the function of maintaining a balance in support for the public service and in the information system where, moreover, newspapers that carry out a fundamental cultural and information function live a phase of great crisis for the competition of the network and for the looting of the online contents, that are then not paid and, therefore, create a difficulty for the publishing companies. And if these companies become impoverished, they will not produce content, and the quality of online information is likely to be worse and worse because it is not produced by real publishing centers. The question of the canon is, therefore, important and complex. Then, proposing to abolish the canon is very easy and is a source of easy popularity. And I think this topic will come back later. Moreover, the real alternative to the fee would be a total privatisation of the RAI, which in this debate some of the amendments propose and which - I would like to remind you - is already provided for by the current rules, which allow for the transfer of branches of the Company or its transformation into a listed holding company. This could yield percentages that would remain by public majority, but that are now being partially privatised, as is happening for Poste, or as happened in the past for Enel or for ENI, who have public control, although minority, but the reference shareholder remains the Treasury, i.e. the State. These routes will also be possible in the future for RAI and will then put the theme of the canon in a different perspective. We understand that the delegation to regulate the canon may also pursue the important objective of charging all and less. I claim to my political side, years ago, the adoption of rules - for example - to exempt citizens above a certain age from payment of the fee, when the use of the public service actually becomes a way of spending time. And for those who receive low pensions, it is also right to be able to enjoy them free of charge. I believe that on the subject of making everyone pay, because there is a large escape, and charging less is found and you can find a sharing. This delegation does not convince us, however, because then we do not know what is being done with it. We would have preferred, therefore, that the delegations, on this and other points, either there were at all or they were limited and had more precise content. It can be said that the delegation serves: there is in fact the vexed question whether to put the fee in the electric bill, because they all receive it and, so in the end it becomes a way to pay. Then there were those who said to abolish the canon and to put it into general taxation: it is a mockery, because it means that then taxes increase by a figure that one tries to disguise in the accumulation of taxes, but in the end people pay the same; therefore, that is a hypothesis of pretend abolition of the canon. A delegation therefore leaves all these questions open. Several governments, even with a centre-right majority, have in the past sought instruments for a fight against the evasion of the canon even pursuing the objective of its lowering, which then is not fair that it pays a higher figure who sets himself up and pays zero who escapes. Here, the delegation frankly does not solve all these problems, but leaves them unsolved. I would also like to say, however, that, in drawing up a deleterious amendment to the article, in addition to a few more details, we have noted that the debate in the Commission has not been pointless. I would like to recall here - although I do not see Senator Rossi present in the Chamber - that with the contribution of various colleagues, much has been discussed about the role of the local broadcasting, so much so that the article has changed its title from the previous draft and has become "Delegate to the Government for the discipline of the financing of the public service and the financing of the local broadcasting." This means that, in the exercise of the delegation, the Government will also have to see how to allocate resources to the local issue, which is experiencing a time of difficulty for the technological transition, the competition of satellites and the multiplication of channels with terrestrial digital, which have sometimes made life difficult, after having known in Italy great seasons. Someone said that there was an excessive number of them, since we had planetary records as to the number of local broadcasters. And not all of them can stand it, because the advertising market is what it is, and in fact a selection took place in the field of larger broadcasters that prevailed over others, which - alas - did not have a dimension to withstand the challenge. I therefore welcome the fact that a reflection has been opened, but we are opposed to an idea of delegation in this area. You see, ladies and gentlemen, of course, we are following the debates more closely than those who are more interested in the various subjects, and therefore we are turning to knowledge or supposed knowledge of the issues. The problems of the canon, of the cost of the public service and of the local broadcasting, however, affect everyone: when you do the statistical surveys and the opinion polls, it turns out that the Carabinieri are the most popular and the canon is the most popular tax, which then amounts to 100 euros, and therefore there are worse ones, as surely those on the house. Then I will propose an amendment on the abolition of delegation, because the issue of the canon, which must certainly be addressed, deserved a detailed public debate as it affects the whole population. Sometimes we discuss laws that concern a segment, a sector, a profession, a field of activity, while the fee concerns all citizens, being essentially a tax matter, a tax of purpose. Moreover, we discussed this when the current government removed 150 million euros from RAI and it was said that it could not do so because it was a tax of purpose. As you can see, the matter is delicate, so it is a mistake to treat it with a proxy. That is why we call for the deletion of Article 4, which deals with a fair subject, but which should be detailed and not delegated too indefinitely. [[Applause from the FI-PdL Group XVII.]]
ParlaMint-IT_2015-07-30-LEG17-Senato-sed-494.ana.u254	Mr President, we too have tabled a deleterious amendment to Article 4, because - as we have also pointed out in the preliminary question of constitutionality - in our opinion the delegation contained in it has no precise criteria and retains its vagueness, despite a particularly sensitive and important subject which deserved, instead, to be excluded from the measure. In fact, as I have happened to repeat with a certain frequency, the text bears a wrong title, because it concerns exclusively governance and not - as it has been pompously titled - the reform of RAI, so much so that of the most important issues concerning the mission of the public service - is the central theme if you really want to make a serious reform - it does not talk about it, but bypasses it, and reduces it to some principle, on agendas. To the limit, therefore, they must be dealt with by another measure. In our opinion, already in the broad delegation that is given is contained a fundamental vice, because the issue of the canon also concerns the financing of the local broadcasting, which is also a very sensitive issue. Above all, the matter of the canon must, if necessary, be treated expressly, not with generic indications, but with a structure that formulated proposals voted by the Senate. And in the subsequent amendments we will go even further on the substance of our proposal. The issue of the canon has always been the subject of discussion and also gives the sign of different conceptions and projects on the RAI. Many amendments have been tabled proposing the abolition of the tax rate; others, like ours, make the alternative proposal, to measure it to income, thus providing for progressiveness, since it is a tax. However, the issue of the canon - in our opinion - had to be dealt with with with the true mission of the public service and did not deserve a delegation, in fact very generic and without clear and determined principles and objectives. It is, that is, a watershed also compared to what must be the future itself or the idea itself about the future of public service and RAI. Moreover, the fee is considered an obnoxious tax and there is a very high level of evasion. Therefore, it deserved an in-depth debate, which I hope we will continue with, and put forward the other amendments. All this tells us that there is a problem. If we compare the fee to the subscription costs of other pay-TVs, it seems to be almost a reduced cost. Yet, it is a tax not loved by citizens and, therefore, it had to be changed again and widely, linking it - as we propose - to income. At the same time, however, it was necessary to associate the users in representation, so much so that we had made a very precise proposal that included, on the board of directors, as well as a representative of the workers, one of the users. Another hypothesis, which you rejected earlier, was to put users' representatives in order with the payment of the fee within the guarantee board. This is an absolutely non-trivial issue and I am quite sorry that at this point, being the subject of a delegation within this article, it is left in its vagueness. Yet, it is one of the issues that deserved to be addressed in a genuine and serious reform of the RAI, or to be well discussed within Article 4 with much more binding, clear and precise delegation criteria and with an indication of the objectives. That is why we think that we should choose between the two. In this particular case, in the face of such a general delegation, I think it is better to delete the article.
ParlaMint-IT_2015-07-30-LEG17-Senato-sed-494.ana.u255	Mr President, many of the remarks just made by Senator De Petris allow me to frame just and specifically some of the amendments we have tabled to this article. We are faced with the umpteenth delegation that deserves a very precise, binding and specific discussion on some parts that have instead opted to expunge, to address them in other aspects and in other legislative activities. In this same article the discipline for the financing of public service and local broadcasting is recalled. We pay great attention to the local broadcasting, but we do not believe this is the place where the Government can decide to make a quick and fury measure, which only identifies in the governance of RAI its perimeter of intervention and then addresses a topic concerning a situation in some ways dramatic and not worthy of a civil country. I am referring to the field of information, the publishing of printed paper, electronics, radio and television, which deserves, despite the many proclamations and commitments, a comparison, an activity and a corresponding legislative production. If this is to be the case, we think it would be better to delete the article, as we propose in our amendment. But with a view to reducing at least the damage, if we have to go to a form of lawmaking, then we begin to establish certain criteria, which must be - as we say above all in Amendments 4,209 and 4,272 - fairness and certainty. We are convinced that the public service needs certainties in terms of resources and cannot be left to the good will - and therefore to the blackmail - of power, of the Commission. When it comes to these issues, other events are often referred to in which the Commission must call for livelihood resources. No, we want to put the public service on our own and protect it. At the same time, however, we must respect fairness because the famous tax of purpose, seeing among the various experiences of European countries what it can be, must have its proportionality, precisely because it is a tax of purpose that risks seeing the highest levels of morosity. There is a risk that, being indistinguished, you will pay "x" regardless of the financial conditions of the citizens. So, I think we need to start right there. It is obvious that it is difficult with the instrument of delegation, because the criteria, parameters, percentages and ranges within which different measures and different tax weights are matched are not indifferent. Therefore, the instrument of delegation, on this very subject, is absolutely inadequate. The arranged combination makes us say this, but at least let us not predict them in such general terms with the usual agenda. The government is given a precise statement by the Senate Chamber which says that if there is to be - and there must be - a levy it must be absolutely proportionate to the financial conditions of the population. Otherwise we think of facing a problem and solving it - I am talking about giving certainty to the public service - but instead we increase evasion because there is no correspondence and sharing.

The current wording of Article 5 (2) (a) reads as follows: "I reorder and simplify the provisions in force also for the purposes of their adaptation, taking into account technological and market developments, without new or increased burdens for public finance." As I had the opportunity to say in the general discussion and as the Undersecretary reassured in his reply (but at this point also in this case paper sings), if he stopped to the words "reorder and simplify the existing provisions" I believe that the problems and doubts that had led to the request for the deletion of paragraph 2 could fail. In other words, I would ask you to return to the title of the article, which reads: "Absence and delegation to the Government for regulatory reorganization" As draftsmen of amendment 5.9, we do not believe that the issues of adaptation, technological and market developments are included in the reform. Here is the heart of the reform of the RAI, which has nothing to do with the reorganization and simplification of the existing provisions. Therefore, if the Government were willing to return to that original wording, I believe that reservations and doubts would be overcome.
ParlaMint-IT_2015-07-30-LEG17-Senato-sed-494.ana.u307	Mr President, I would like to give some advice. What you are learning is that much of the reform is about these two issues: the first is about the delegation on the canon and the second is what we are talking about now. I am convinced that, compared to the debate that has taken place, it would be much more logical to postpone the examination of a reform which continues to be very limited than the problems of the whole sector. This would also make sense of everything that has been done in the Supervisory Committee. I am convinced that right now - on this I disagree with others - RAI needs its own government. The extension has now gone on for two months and therefore it is necessary to give a government to RAI in a short time. For this very reason, however, I believe that at this point it is necessary, since there is no longer that impellence, to give space and breath to a reform that risks being nick, that is to say to arrive as a measure that only concerns governance that, among other things, is carried out with an old law, without addressing the main nodes of the sector. For this reason I ask the Government if it is possible, because the essential fact at this stage is the government of RAI, to postpone in part or to give much more breath to the comparison on the provision of law. It is clear, in fact, that when we find ourselves giving a government to RAI, it will be the moment when we understand, according to the names that will be chosen, the value that will be given, the type of personality that will be chosen, the ability of this Parliament to make a turning point for the Public Service Company. This would help, because on the one hand you would do what some - not me - define a forced force, taking names that - it is what counts - demonstrate that they want to move on and, on the other, would lengthen the times of the reform of the system trying to insert the nerve themes of the public service. ParlaMint-IT_2015-07-30-LEG17-Senato-sed-494.ana.u325	Gasparri (FI-PdL XVII). Mr President, we have reached the amendments referred to Article 5. I would like to speak on Amendment 5.3, which proposes the deletion of Article 5 and which is identical to Amendments 5.4, first signed by Senator Cioffi, 5,200, first signed by Senator Cervellini, 5,201, first signed by Senator Airola, and 5,202, first signed by Senator Crosio. I would like to dwell for a moment on the issue of delegation, because it is a delicate point of the affair, together with the issue of the managing director-general (now there is a law in force that does not provide for a managing director, that there will be when and if this law will be passed). These are the points which we have raised in general debate and on which we reserve the right to speak. With this amendment, we call for the deletion of Article 5 and the delegation; other groups also do the same thing. We will therefore certainly vote in favour of this amendment and then see what happens. I would like to take this opportunity, since there has been a sort of general mini-discussion on the rewording of other amendments relating to delegation, to underline the fact that the rapporteur, using the rewording of other amendments that come forward, has limited the scope of this delegation. I say this because the discussions that we have here are also part of the interpretation of the laws; I draw in particular the attention of the Undersecretary Giacomelli, whom I thank for his speech. While the drafting of Article 5 which we are now examining in this House, coming from the Commission, is talking about reordering and simplifying the existing provisions, also with a view to adapting them, taking into account technological developments, the rewording limits this delegation to the tasks of the public service, with regard to the various technological platforms. Therefore, the delegation is narrower and concerns public service and technological developments. Moreover, the RAI has not so far objectively used to the best the opportunities that new technologies allow in the interaction of television, the Internet, TV on demand and everything that today goes in the technological convergence, that the law in force - I say incidentally - has anticipated creating the integrated system of communication (SIC), that is, the whole of all the various television and journalistic realities. It was contested, but we anticipated what happened later. We therefore call on the House to vote in favour of our deletion of the delegation. In taking up the previous speech, the House has in some way, with transversality and with votes not of all parties, circumscribed this desire of the Government to have delegations and deleted Article 4, with a delegation on the canon. It seems to me that now the Government wisely does not give up the delegation, but limits it to public service and technological evolution, without having a blank delegation for the rewriting of the norms, because here we talk about reordering and simplification. Simplification means that there may be repetitive rules in the single text or that there may be overlaps. The legislation in the field of radio and television goes back to different stages of Italian history, so sometimes there are stratifications. We therefore interpret the debate in this way, Undersecretary Giacomelli: to limit this aspect of the public service of technological evolution in terms of reordering and simplification, not in terms of delegation in white. I also say this to those who spoke earlier. I am referring to the intervention of Senator Mineo, who has joined in some way on this aspect, without wishing to give a blank delegation, which is not there, which is restricted and which would have been in conflict with the role of Parliament and with the will of the House, which has also been expressed a moment ago. In this sense, we confirm the deleterious amendment of the article, but we note that the suppressive will is leading to a dynamic of rewording behind the delegations, restricting them in some way, which seems wise and useful to me. I say this for the sake of clarity, because in the past few weeks an inadequate communication has caused fireflies to pass through the lanterns and has somewhat confused the debate that has taken place in the Commission. Having said that, we confirm that it is always better to abolish this delegation. [[Applause from the FI-PdL Group XVII.]]

Moreover, if you think of RAI as something that still has to project into the future, we can guarantee a historical memory to young people, that yes, it is true, they are very connected to platforms that are not the TV ones but, in the perspective of a use of these contents, an idea could be to make them available to those who are subscribers on the Internet, as if it were an archive to Netflix, really exterminated, accessible to those who paid the fee. This could be an idea. So this point is fundamental precisely for the identity of the Italian people and of the RAI Italian radio and television.

ParlaMint-IT_2015-07-30-LEG17-Senato-sed-494.ana.u449	Mr President, several amendments to Article 2 re-examine the issue of the power of the Managing Director and Director-General.
