ParlaMint-IT_2015-07-30-LEG17-Senato-sed-494.ana.u450	Madam President, I shall go into detail, amendment by amendment, in the explanation of vote. Surely Article 2 covers the whole question of the heart of what we propose. There is an entirely replacement amendment which provides for the reform of the AGCOM which, in our draft law, had to function as the first point for verification and for the function of the steering booth, which we can make him carry out even without reforming it. A transparent procedure for the submission of candidates for the Management Board and for the role of CEO is also provided for. This is because we believe that the first, the founding, point for a new RAI consists of three basic requirements. The first relates to good repute, that is to say, the fact that a candidate has no criminal background, has not been sentenced and is not in a legal state which is not appropriate to the role he or she should play. The second is independence, which was the fundamental point of our bill. Moreover, this point saw similar, in the proposal of the Renzi Government and in that of the 5 Star Movement, the two aspects: that is, a very slim board of directors. Simply, while the Premier predicted that this board of directors, very slim, was appointed and dependent on the Government and the parties, we instead applied a very simple equation: the greater operation of a slim board of directors is directly proportional to its greater independence. Clearly, as we build control equipment on it, we slow down the decision-making process. The characteristics of RAI, inherent in the fact that it is a huge company, which must be reformed almost from the foundations, in some ways - which must be done quickly - required a very effective ability to operate and agility of the board of directors. Unfortunately, in the Commission, we were faced with a basic text of the draft law starting from that of the government, which did not provide for this independence. We then tabled a number of amendments, which we have written and reworded specifically - but I will present them to you later, in the explanation of vote, because you will have to be careful - which relate to these positive and negative requirements. Among the positive requirements - I repeat - there are the competences, identified by areas: we must think that RAI is a society that has technologies, therefore, there must be technological skills, but also editorial skills, because it is a cultural industry, as well as legal and managerial, because a board of directors does that. Among the negative requirements, however, we include fundamental concepts, first of all the independence of candidates: no candidate, as we had foreseen in our legal text, can enter the office of board of directors if in the last five years he has been in Parliament and, in politics or in the secretariat of a party and, if he has therefore held elected or governmental positions. This was immediately seen as an outrage to Parliament by many, but not by all; on the contrary, many have told me that it was an excellent proposal, which I will explain later in detail. After that, there were other negative requirements, which I will explain later. Another fundamental aspect that President Tarantola reminded us yesterday, during the hearing in the RAI Supervisory Committee, is about transparent procedures. One thing that we consider essential is that the proposals and curricula of candidates are collected, as well as a description of how they intend to implement the public service, with a transparent procedure, which is then published, visible and accessible on the Internet. I have mentioned President Tarantola because - as you said - transparent procedures correspond to effective and democratic procedures, which tend above all to eliminate the plague in RAI, that is, the mediocrity of the recommended, which puts the whole system in crisis. If we could have a comprehensive transparency plan - and I have to say that some things have been transposed, which I am pleased about - the bill would already begin to improve. Although it is controlled by politics and government, the new board of directors would start to invest in the profiles of competence, which is what it needs first of all. The CEO, then, is a big problem, because Italy is the only country that would see a public service in which the shareholders' meeting decides directly who it is. Not only that: the managing director has many powers, but on this - I repeat - we are also in agreement, because, if one must reform a company, it must be operational; the problem, however, is its independence. Many people have told us, believe me: Ingrid Deltenre, Director-General of the EBU (European Broadcasting Union), who manages and brings together all European public services, with a billion spectators. And if you tell us, I'd stretch an ear. Professor Cheli, in a hearing at the Supervisory Committee, told us that it is not unconstitutional to predict that politicians do not set foot in RAI for a time. Perhaps, however, it is not constitutional for the government to decide directly who it will administer, as well as its editorial lines and internal appointments in the field of public radio and television service. This is expected in Moldova and Bulgaria. That is unacceptable. Starting to conclude, the votes are also important, because in the text the four candidates expressed by Chamber and Senate are elected with a vote limited to one. This implies that, with a large majority prize, in the face of very fragmented oppositions, the majority - if you like - is the en plein, in the sense that it agrees. Let us say that the majority award to the House is 340 Members, predicting that the Senate will disappear, 170 vote one candidate, 170 vote another, oppositions must reach that number or at least approach. If at every vote the majority is defeated and the oppositions do not agree - imagine putting in agreement oppositions so far away on some visions - it means that the CEO is not only appointed by the Government, but the entire board of directors is in the hands of the majority. This can happen now because you are there, but - as I have said several times about constitutional reforms - it is not said that this despotic system that is building tomorrow does not belong to someone else who wins the elections. But I reserve the right to continue. We therefore proposed a vote with a two-thirds majority, which at least provides for an agreement between the parties and a sharing. After all, it is the system that to date guarantees the maximum sharing between political forces.
ParlaMint-IT_2015-07-30-LEG17-Senato-sed-494.ana.u451	Madam President, on matters of parliamentary technique we have followed another order, but I would like to point out to my colleagues that, with Article 2, we are at the heart of the draft law: in fact, I would remind you that the measure involves the reform of governance and Article 2 essentially redesigns the governance of the RAI. I will divide my speech into two parts. Firstly, I claim with a bit of pride that the real innovative system, the real reformative action was contained in the draft law that I had presented and which was then transformed into Amendment 2.6. All the more so, after the discussion in the Commission and in the Chamber, I come out even more strengthened in the conviction that the real turning point of the RAI passed from the dual system, that is, from a clearer distinction between the power of direction and control, entrusted to the supervisory board, and the power of management, entrusted to a management board, with an independent president, a managing director with full powers and with three other senior executives of the Company, on the classic dual model of Nordic matrix. I am sorry that Mr Giacomelli is not here, but I do not reveal any secrets - the rapporteur knows that - if I say that there has also been a debate on this issue within the PD Group in the Supervisory Committee. I am aware that in the end the Government's choice has gone somewhere else, that is, towards substantial maintenance - the term "mechanical" is given to me - of the Gasparri law. I would ask him, however, for a symbolic availability in relation to the transformation of amendment 2.6 on the dual system into an agenda on which he is committed to assessing the appropriateness of using the dual system when - I hope soon - he will address the issue of a more comprehensive reform of the RAI, and not only of governance. I would therefore like to ask the rapporteur essentially whether he is prepared to turn the agenda into an opportunity. There are also two other issues that I would like to highlight, given that I am now announcing the withdrawal of amendment 2.117, which concerns the sources of appointment of the President and the CEO of the RAI. As far as the President is concerned, in the work of the Commission, in an effort which the Government has made and which I give him, there has been a search for a solution that takes into account a President of guarantee. I have the impression and, therefore, the doubt that in the end the solution found is somehow a step back and not forward. At this point, if you wanted a President of guarantee, you could go towards the solution of a President appointed, as has already been for the past, in agreement between the Presidents of the House and the Senate. We have put the proposal on the table and of course we will wait for the rapporteur's assessment of this and then we will comply. There is one last issue that I personally hold very much and is the subject of the source of appointment of the managing director, on which the confrontation was absolutely loyal and also profitable with the rapporteur. It is true that in the model of classic companies, even more so of companies of the size of RAI, the source of appointment is directly the shareholder who indicates the head of the company. But I would point out that the panorama of a somewhat atypical object, i.e. state televisions, by a public majority, tells us that this is not the case. In the hearing of the director of the EBU, the models for the appointment of the heads of companies and the managing director were indicated at this time. There are basically two: the first is a BBC model, in which there is a division of ownership, and therefore the State no longer has direct ownership and entrusts to a trust, to a foundation the appointment of the heads of companies; the second, widespread in all the main European nations and the European Broadcasting Union (EBU), with the exception of Bulgaria, is the board, that is the board of directors that appoints the managing director. With the exception of Bulgaria, there is no direct appointment of the CEO. I observe in the text of the Government in some way a contradiction, that is the Government says that the managing director is appointed by the board of directors, on proposal of the shareholders' meeting, that is of the Government, that is in fact is a nomination of the Government. I believe that, having the Government the possibility of appointment, both in the new model, and in the model that we have in some way enshrined and provided for in the current Gasparri law, one can identify the power of direction of the shareholder, which I share, in the expression of one of the two advisors who are appointed by the Government on the board of directors. At that point it is the board of directors that appoints the managing director. There is, therefore, no direct appointment. I realize that this is a matter of a formal rather than a substantive nature, but I hope that I have been able to explain that there is a logic behind which, in an atypical company, a public good, a piece of the country's material Constitution as RAI is, there is a clearer distinction between the power of the Executive and the power of Parliament. I feel, from this point of view, at this stage, that I am continuing to insist with the Government and the rapporteur that this element should go in the direction of our amendment, which - I repeat - has nothing revolutionary or obstructive towards the Government. It is simply taking photographs of the best European practices, if not of almost all the practices of the major European public service companies, to apply them also to RAI.

ParlaMint-IT_2015-07-30-LEG17-Senato-sed-494.ana.u453	Madam President, as Senator Cervellini has already explained, the amendments we have tabled to this article are of two kinds. Since in our country the discussion on what may be the nomination mechanisms (so the governance of the boards of directors) has been going on for a long time and since it for the truth, in past times, has also passionate about the best intelligences and opinions makers of this country, we thought that the fundamental question to be addressed was to introduce a kind of gap between the Company and the different powers, especially those of the parties. For this reason, the main question we raised was to ensure that the Supervisory Committee disappeared and that, on its behalf, but with a very different capacity to represent society and its plural bodies, a Council for Guarantees in the public service was set up, which, in its definition, I believe is very much in the sense of what we mean. It is a guarantee board for the public service composed in an absolutely articulated way. I talked about it earlier, in article 4, about the canon, explaining that the canon is seen as a tax in some way not loved by citizens, because one has never had the courage to call users themselves to a control and participation in the dynamics, management and address of the company. We have found the solution to introduce them into the Guarantee Board, as well as representatives of other associations that I am not here to list. The meaning is very clear: to subtract RAI from the parties and the dynamics of lottification, leaving certainly a pluralistic political representation, but also inserting one of the country and civil society and, however, of all those who should have the possibility to say their own and also to bring their own experience (I used to speak of producers, artists, directors and all the information operators). It is the guarantee board, in our proposal, that appoints the board of directors; it is the famous intercapedine that fully guarantees the pluralism and independence of the service of the public company. What we find here - which is then the heart and the true purpose of this bill, that is to intervene on governance - proposes a completely different model: a very strong control, on the part of the Government, first of all on the board of directors, because in the end we create a director (which is what decides most) composed of the president and two directors of administration, expression of the shareholder, and therefore of the Government. Then there's the CEO's vulnus. In our case, however, the guarantee board, which is the intercapedine, appoints the board of directors, which then appoints the director-general. In our proposal we also address the question of the professionalism, skills and transparency with which members are chosen, asking the question not only of incompatibility, but also of their profile, through the proposal to set up a register at the Authority to register and submit applications and curricula to be consulted, so that the process is transparent and the selection is linked to professionalism. I do not dwell any further on the issue of the establishment of the legislation. It is clear, however, that we have tried to transfer this approach to a number of amendments, which also seek to include, in some way, strong elements of correction which - I am sorry to say - have not been taken into account. We have expressed our attempt to correct the plant through the submission of amendments, proposing the presence on the board of directors also of a representative of the users, to be accompanied by the representative of the workers. We have also indicated a profile of the Chairman of the Board of Directors, with precise features, through the establishment of a register with the Authority for the guarantees in communications. We have put the same question very strongly by bringing back to the board the appointment not of the CEO, but of the Director-General, for the issues we have addressed here, also in the debate on the sub-amendments to the amendment tabled by the rapporteur in Article 6. You've never seen such a thing: nothing but subtract RAI from parties! Here the RAI is placed directly in the hands of the Government and the majority. Let us think about what will happen in the future, when the Italicum comes into force, and the overwhelming majority of the board of directors, equal to two thirds, and the CEO will be the direct expression of the majority and the government. This involves a vulnus to the idea of independence, also due to the fact that it has not seriously addressed a debate on the public service. It is a very dangerous vulnus, which, when it ever comes into force, will also have consequences from the point of view of European legislation. The effort we have made has been to indicate a model that was the best one in Europe. We never imagined that we would consider Bulgaria's. I would like to remind you that Italy already has some problems with the ranking on freedom of information, around the seventieth or seventy-twond place. We would have needed everything, except to go and choose a model - in our opinion - authoritarian and dangerous. In addition, with the delegation provided for in Article 4, the operation was also carried out not to give certainty about resources. I believe that, from this point of view and with this facility, independence not only comes out humiliated, but is in danger of being completely questioned. [[Applause from Senator Bignami.]]

There is no contradiction with the approach of the Constitutional Court's judgments. The relationship between the board of directors and the managing director must be assessed as a whole. It is true that, as today there is a proposal of the shareholder, it is also true that the board of directors appoints and revokes - can revoke - the managing director. So, in our opinion, there is a balanced system of powers. However, neither on this puto nor on any other we are unavailable to an open comparison that will examine every possibility and verify all points. We weren't in the Senate, and we won't be in the next steps. Since the beginning of this path, the Government has shown that that caricature image of the armored position does not correspond to reality. An invitation to withdraw, made on these points and with this premise not to stiffen the confrontation unnecessarily, I do not think it represents a problem. During the vote, and since we have further steps before us to reflect and compare, I believe that the withdrawal of the amendments concerning the sources of appointment and this report, starting with Senator Fornaro's amendment 2.6 (which has explained his, but has given voice to a position that those who took part in the Commission's work can be diffused), allows us to avoid, simply, stiffening positions. That is why I would like to point out the call for the withdrawal of the amendments which concern these points. Otherwise, the opinion is in line with the rapporteur's opposite if the invitation is not accepted.

ParlaMint-IT_2015-07-30-LEG17-Senato-sed-494.ana.u494	CIOFFI (M5S). Mr President, this amendment essentially proposes in a more or less complete way the original proposal which reflects our idea of what the RAI should be. So I wanted to talk about it, because basically the heart of our original proposal, the thing that we consider more and more consistent with the final objective that we had given, that was to free RAI from the parties, was the mode of appointment of the members of the board of directors. We had proposed, and we continue to think that the optimal solution was that the appointment of members would take place through a ban that was to be called by AGCOM, as we have repeatedly said, that it would select a number of people with certain very strict characteristics. This is in order to create a grid from which to choose a number of names that would then become the members of the board of directors, through what we have called a draw, which seemed to us the only way that could really eliminate the indications from the parties. Obviously what will happen on Tuesday, with the appointment of the new board of directors with the current law, will be nothing more than the perpetuation of what has always been and unfortunately also what will be. We really felt that that was the only way, also because, as we have already said in general debate, there was no proposal for another, to free RAI from the parties. We tried to ask someone if there was a different and alternative way, and as we have already said in general discussion, this is essentially the same way that we have identified to choose the director of the work, for example, of a great public work, therefore in the system of concessions. In that case we did exactly that; we said that we would call for a ban and we would choose by drawing a draw from a grid of people of recognized abilities to whom we entrust the management of the works. If you can use this system for a large public work that can cost several billion euros, you don't understand why it doesn't have to be valid to choose a member of RAI's board of directors. We continue to believe that this would be the optimal solution. Then there are a number of amendments that put a brake and a few stakes in place. These are the famous stakes that a person who was also president of RAI told us about, who was overly mocked by a satirical program. However, the stakes are the minimum for when Parliament is going to vote; when that happens, because, as we have already said, this law will come into force, if it ever comes into force, after we have made the new board of directors with the current law. These posts are the minimum indispensable to choose the members of the board of directors. Of course, this amendment also talks about other issues on which the rapporteur has spoken and which he has partially accepted. For example, it also states that all contracts exceeding EUR 2.5 million are subject to the opinion of the Management Board. Instead, in the bill this stake was raised to 10 million euros. Since currently the amount is about 2,582,000 euros, it has not been understood why you want to raise this stake; perhaps it is always to give this great power to the managing director. Perhaps it is the case that on these contracts, which are important, important and substantial, there is a specific opinion, also because later on we will see that we have left the possibility of assigning work below the Community threshold even without competition. This is also a point that we believe is incorrect because it is contrary to what we have said about the procurement code. It is not clear why the RAI can entrust, without making the contest, a contract of works for an amount up to 5.150.000 euros. This is also a particular vulnus. Then there are a number of other things that we'll talk about later. However, it was important to make this statement on this amendment because, being the heart of our proposal, we believe it is important to reiterate, once again, what is our optimal solution. I do not think this is our optimal solution; I think it is an optimal solution, but since there has not been another one, it is the only one that we have encountered in this large number of amendments. That is why we wanted to take action to reaffirm our position. At times, as a Group, our position is held by someone who may be a little eltranzist, rigid or radical. I do not want to use the word utopia in this House because it is important and would require a lot more time to discuss it, however, sometimes remembering to tend to the dream and the possibility of feeling beyond tactics and the pure and a little sad and, perhaps, miserable partition, would allow us to do a better job, to be different and finally, perhaps, to fly with wings. Those great wings would perhaps bring us a different Italy. This is also this RAI reform since it is the tool that is used to do all the things we talked about in the mission on Article 1. Perhaps this Parliament should learn to spread its wings and feel free like a seagull. But sometimes, we believe that perhaps the only way is that the Five Star Movement goes to rule; when we rule we will free our wings and all those who have broken them will weep.

ParlaMint-IT_2015-07-30-LEG17-Senato-sed-494.ana.u513	CIOFFI (M5S). Mr President, this amendment basically proposes something that we will then find in another amendment later, again on Article 2: these are the requirements that we consider important for the appointment of the members of the Management Board. In fact, some criteria are set out in Article 49, paragraph 4, of the law in force. However, we believe that the requirements of the members of the board that we propose are much clearer and more stringent, even if they can be identified as non-exclusive requirements. Indeed, the current wording of the requirements is quite general. This non-tendant definition, and therefore not very specific in terms of the characteristics and competences of the members of the board of directors, leaves more room for manoeuvre and greater scope for manoeuvre. Since Tuesday, as we have already said, we will have to go and name the members of the board with the old law and since there is this great availability (to name them Tuesday, probably many names are already in the minds, not to mention in the pockets, of those who will then have to choose these names), I think it's good that in this case too we start to put some 'hands in the dish and start to choose what are the characteristics. We proposed a series of characteristics, calibrated on our hypothesis and on our project, which was that - as we said before - to identify five components, precisely to have a slim, efficient and effective board of directors. The words "efficiency" and "effectiveness" always come back to us from a law that has been important in this country for those who cared like me about water, the Galli law, which spoke of efficiency, effectiveness and economics. It is therefore necessary to have a board of directors that is really effective in being able to identify and direct the RAI towards its new mission and it is good that it is composed of specific people and with very clear characteristics. During a speech we had with the rapporteur and the government, we also talked about not making these characteristics exclusive. We thought that maybe this could be a way to meet halfway through, in that speech that was also made by the Undersecretary, but leaving these characteristics, even if not exclusive. Of course, we believe that these characteristics are much more specific than those provided for by the current law and perhaps we should try to throw the heart beyond the obstacle. That is why we have tabled this amendment, which we shall also be discussing later. The request is to try to vote on it; perhaps you can think of rewording it, but the request is to try to vote on it.

Since we have been opposed to the possibility of considering immediately excluding from the Board of Directors of RAI all those who held an elective office, particularly Members of Parliament, and we know that on more than one occasion they have been elected to the Board of Directors of RAI of politicians and have made a mess, I would like to say, first of all, that we do not want to diminish the role of the Member of Parliament. Here we are talking about an independent power, that of information, which is a bit like the judiciary: certain rules that apply to the judiciary should also apply to information. Therefore no one wants to diminish the role of parliamentarians, but objectively there has been an exaggerated presence of politicians, who have entered directly on the Board of Directors of RAI. We're not saying they never have to do this, but we can't do it after a whole lot of time. It seems to me to be a basic concept, especially in the light of the statement: Get the parties out of the RAI. The fact that he cannot be appointed on the Board of Directors of RAI who has just left an elected or governmental office or a party secretariat seems to me to be common sense and shared. By the way, I've been talking to many of you individually, and many of you have told me that it's a good thing. This is also said by constitutionalist Enzo Cheli, according to whom it is not an anti-constitutional norm, but rather a norm to be promoted, because it is the only way not to propose mediocrity in RAI, but competence. Mr President, my speech was merely intended to underline the fact that, in this Parliament, in this Senate (as long as it exists), we are constantly using the exercise of derogation. And since the words "deroga", "exception" or synonyms in the collection of all the laws of Italy and regionals appear 60,000 times, it will be appropriate to start writing laws without making exceptions.

ParlaMint-IT_2015-07-30-LEG17-Senato-sed-494.ana.u555	I ask you to speak for an explanation of vote.
ParlaMint-IT_2015-07-30-LEG17-Senato-sed-494.ana.u556	You have the right to do so.
ParlaMint-IT_2015-07-30-LEG17-Senato-sed-494.ana.u557	CIOFFI (M5S). Mr President, here we return to the previous speech, namely the characteristics that the members of the Management Board must have. I understand that the rapporteur wanted to give the opposite opinion, because in theory there is a contrast to the characteristics of the law in force, but let us return to the first point, namely that the members of the board of directors must have specific characteristics, because they must consider and identify the role of RAI in the future. Why not then think that they should, for example, be subject to specific managerial experience, in public or private enterprises, or experts in the audiovisual production area with at least five years of managerial experience in the broadcasting sectors and activities. Why not? That sounds like really common sense to me. Or they could be subject to technical-scientific skills that have gained at least managerial experience in public or private enterprises. In short, we believe that the members of the board of directors must have specific experiences, considering that they go to head RAI, not just any company. A construction company is not the same as RAI, nor are ANAS or Ferrovie dello Stato Italiane or any other public company. Each company has specific characteristics: can we ever think that directing ANAS and directing RAI is the same thing? We think not and therefore we believe that certain specific characteristics are necessary, because we need to know well the environment that must be governed: how else can we have a vision? How can we think that the president of ANAS can do one who does not have a specific competence, but who perhaps, even with all due respect, is competent in philosophy, for example? For goodness sake, worthy people, with great capacity and level, but perhaps you need to have a vision of what an infrastructure is, to give an example. Why, then, is this example not appropriate as regards the members of the RAI Board of Directors? We continue to ask ourselves this, because once again, as we have already said, we are faced with the appointment of the new members of the board of directors on Tuesday. We've said this before: God forbid anyone who may be sitting in this House or in the House should be appointed to the RAI board of directors, because we do not think that is the case. Once someone would have called it the political opportunity not to go through those sliding doors that Senator Airola told you about earlier. So, if we think that this action is not correct, we think it is much more correct to identify people of great capacity and quality, precisely because they need to know the market in which they operate. I take a trivial example: how can you imagine evaluating the editorial line on scientific programs someone who has never seen a documentary or imagine how RAI should develop in the field of cable communications? We have already said that the technological transformation related to fiber optics will bring Italy in a different condition and RAI cannot fail to participate in this challenge. So how can we think that on the RAI's board of directors there is no one with specific skills in this field? We have already talked about Netflix's landing in Italy and we have already said that in this country it has never been possible to develop a cable television because there was conflict with that gentleman, with that being called Silvio Berlusconi, who had private televisions and that, as has already been said clearly by the members of my Group, it has also been appropriate of RAI doing the "ace pigliattutto": from Campania I allow myself to use this expression, because this has been done. How, then, can we put a stop to this, if we do not choose people of high quality and competence, of high and specific capacity, who have not played a role in this Parliament? There are probably some very worthy people in this Parliament, maybe some outside the 5 Star Movement, for Christ's sake, but is it possible to think that a person who has played a role here as a legislator will then be running the RAI? Senator Airola has told you well about the power that RAI has in influencing citizens, so it is good that the functions of those who have to legislate and those who have to do something else, which is the cultural change of this country, are deeply detached. That is why we believe that the characteristics that the members of the Management Board must have must be specific; perhaps not exclusive, we have talked about this with the rapporteur and with the Government. It's okay that they're not exclusive, but some solid poles are needed, because otherwise, as usual, we are in this magnum sea in which everyone splashes. However, since we don't like to splash, because we know that you can splash even in the mud, perhaps it is good that these fish swim in the free sea and are cleaned in clean water. So, in order to be able to splash in clean water, you have to make sure that the water is really clean and that these people are fish; not piscitielli (as they say from my side, of the straw piscitielli), but important fish that can feel deeply free: to feel free, they must be people of great quality and have specific skills, because otherwise the shark will come on duty to eat them. We want the members of the board to be strong, authoritative people and not to be servants of someone who tells them what to do, otherwise the RAI will never be free. This is the problem of giving specific characteristics to the members of the board of directors: to be free, so that they have the strength to say no to someone who blows them in the ear asking them to do this pleasure or this one, because the pleasure they do only to the citizens. ParlaMint-IT_2015-07-30-LEG17-Senato-sed-494.ana.u576	AIROLA (M5S). Mr President, who knows who you have in mind as CEO, since you did not vote for the exclusion of politics. Know that, even by approving that previous amendment, a good result could have been achieved. Amendment 2.359 instead requires the appointment of the Chairman of the Management Board to be carried out by the Board within the framework of its members. It is expected, however, that the favourable opinion, expressed by a majority of two thirds of its members, will be obtained from the Parliamentary Commission for the general direction and supervision of broadcasting services. We wanted to abolish this Commission, but since it is decided not to make the Management Board independent, it must of course be kept alive. It is clear, in fact, that the more one colonizes himself, through party infiltration or, as I said before, of low-level and mediocre profiles (which are generally those who make a career), the more necessary - at least - a very strong parliamentary control. This will certainly increase the "impedance" of the board of directors and the managing director in carrying out their tasks. However, having no possibility of independence, it is clear that this is how control is carried out. The amendment in question is moving in this direction, also because, from a certain point of view, as I have heard very often repeating in vogue the so-called Gasparri law (so abjured by the President of the Council Renzi), today we have to counteract this power with a parliamentary power. We didn't like it: we wanted a truly independent board of directors and we wouldn't need so many hats, controls, blocks and consultations. It worked well, like a real, working company. Evidently few people here have any idea what it's like to be the CEO of an impressive company like RAI. We need to take a step back. We have already made our observations on the vote limited to one. The amendment provides for a two-thirds majority. It is true that the board of directors, even if it intervened (because it could decide whether or not to confirm the appointment of the managing director), would still be in the hands of the majority. We have already seen it because, out of seven members, six good or bad members are appointed by politics (two by the Government, two by the House and two by the Senate). We have already seen that with the vote limited to one we can, if we are in the majority and if the majority decides it, take all or almost all members. So in the end it is not a problem for Renzi, in his vision as principal of the school, imagining a Plenipotentiary also in RAI, that the CEO can be confirmed by his own board of directors. A faithful majority of the rest already has it: today it has raised the ridge a little, but who knows. The other aspect is the only control that can be opposed to all this: the RAI Supervisory Commission. For God's sake, we were lucky enough to have absolutely worthy presidents in the Commission, but there were also unworthy people who made sure that in any case the Bulgarian edict passed and that in the end politics went beyond its borders and came to work on the editorial line that RAI must have, so also on the conductors and evidently also on the last of the goalkeepers of that company. Amendment 2,359, a bit in the wake of what some of my colleagues were proposing, contains a limitation to the premier's overpower. I make an announcement: in my opinion it will come out sooner or later, because even in the House you said that you will reopen the speech, repeating that this is a law that will probably disappear into thin air, because in three years who knows what situation we will be in. After all, it is only interested that the Director-General takes over the powers of the CEO: at the end of the day this is what interests him. However, if true democratic control still makes sense, we note that Italy would be the only country to have the direct CEO expression of the Premier, the Government, the Presidency of the Council, which we have repeated until exhaustion. This can of course also be constitutional according to your advisors, who perhaps are those of Porcellum, the Giovanardi-Fini law and all the scumbags who have passed through Parliament and who then turned out to be unconstitutional after years, even if at the moment they seemed absolutely lawful. Well, this is one of those cases there. Since we copy so much from Europe, it is clear that in Europe we are the only ones who choose this path. We're also the only ones who still have another anomaly. In this regard, I say to you: look, because that is why we proposed absolute independence from Parliament and from the parties. In Italy we continue to have an anomaly that no one has solved in the last twenty years, that of a former President of the Council, currently still at the head of a large party (in fading, but still a party), which owns television. This has obviously been forgotten, at least in this Parliament. [[Applause from the M5S Group.]]

Imagine that RAI gives 190 million euros, mainly to five companies, with even poor results. Without removing anything from Bernabei's LUX Vide and various fictions such as "Don Matteo," these are products that, objectively, are not saleable, even considering that we are talking about media companies. The only way to limit this damage is transparency. And from the first day that I arrived at the RAI Supervisory Committee, I asked for everything, even past things that no longer even entered into possible conflicts of interest.

Mr President, we have long discussed and tried to specify this passage which is important to us and concerns the members of the Management Board. PRESIDENT. - The debate is We have discussed it "long" before, but now we have to do it briefly. CIOFFI (M5S). Yes, Mr President, in fact, I was referring to the fact that in previous hours we have said that it is essential that the members of the Management Board should not be persons sitting in this House, as we have said, or in the House. This, as we have already said, has probably not been accepted by the House because someone - and we will see what happens on Tuesday - is planning to vote on a name that could be that of a member of this House or that of the House. If, however, we do not want to do this for the members of the Management Board, the amendment before us asks you to do it at least for the CEO: let us ensure that at least he has not held elective public office. I think it is really the minimum trade union, as is often said, since then, when this law is passed, it will have all those famous superpowers: let us see that we approve it in this branch of Parliament; then I want to see what we are going to do with the position of Director-General, who will have the powers of the CEO, to someone who was sitting in this Chamber. The problem is that if, on Tuesday, when we go to vote, at least the law passed by this branch of Parliament does not foresee it, at least it will have saved its face. We therefore ask that we have the courage to approve this: at least, when it happens that the CEO will be someone who has held elective positions in the seven years before the appointment, this will be contrary to what this branch of Parliament has approved. We are really talking about the minimum, that is the fact that the managing director - therefore the general manager, who will have the powers of the managing director - is not one who sits in here. Mr President, I think that this is really the least we owe to the correctness of thought and to the fact that we are consistent between what we do and what we say. So let's try to put it, this little stake for the CEO. That is what this amendment says, nothing more and nothing less. I therefore think it is appropriate to vote on this amendment, because it is indeed appropriate to vote on it: I do not want to repeat myself, Mr President, but the thought of meeting with a Director-General like myself, Senator Cioffi - which, of course, will never happen, but I say this out of absurdity, not to mention the names of others - terrifies me somewhat. If it terrifies me, I think it will terrify anyone in this House. I therefore think it is appropriate that no one should feel so terrified by this idea of jumping and perhaps unhealthy and that this stake should therefore be placed. I want to see what it is with then Tuesday, when we know the names, the board of directors will be able to choose as managing director - indeed, as general manager, because we are always referring to the law in force - a person who sits in this Chamber or in that of the House. I therefore ask all the members of this Parliament to have the courage to say "I do not want to do so and, since I do not want to do so, I am voting in favour of this amendment." To do this, we must have the opportunity to have a person on the market who is perhaps very capable, always in our vision of politics, that I do not know if it is that of the gasparrian policy of ancient memory. We will be transeunte, but be quiet: in two thousand years you will still have Gasparri, if you continue like this. However, having said that, it is important that the MEF and the President-in-Office of the Council, i.e. those who will then seek a suitable figure, have the opportunity to pay it properly, because RAI is a serious patient and needs a great doctor.

ParlaMint-IT_2015-07-30-LEG17-Senato-sed-494.ana.u637	D'ALI' (FI-PdL XVII). Mr President, we must not be confused between populism and issues of rules. We have established in two successive decisions of the financial laws of 2011 and 2014 that publicly controlled companies were subject to a ceiling of remuneration, except those listed on the stock exchange or authorized to issue bond instruments on regulated financial markets. The RAI has always been inside the roofed subsidiaries, then it happened that in the last year the RAI has issued bonds and this would make it escape the ceiling of compensation and liberalize it according to the discretion of the assembly. I believe that, having given absolutely publicistic nature, reiterated also with this reform, to RAI and being the RAI under absolute control of the public administration of the treasure, we must not give RAI, just because it made a bond issue, the possibility to escape the roof established for all other companies under public control. This is the subject that we have also raised in the Budget Committee, on which we have been discussing for a long time. As I recall, the Budget Committee was absolutely oriented towards keeping the RAI in the ranks of publicly controlled companies, thus subject to the ceiling of compensation. It's not about whether it's good or bad or what people think about it, but it's a matter of rules and since when we set the rules, RAI was part of that prediction, I think it has to stay there. I therefore believe that it should not take advantage, just because it has issued a bond, of this escapism in order to release the obligation to maintain the ceiling laid down for all other publicly controlled companies. That is why I think that colleagues want to consider, beyond the easy populisms, Amendment 2,784. I would like to understand from the rapporteur why there is no favourable opinion. Is there the government's will to release the RAI from the ceiling of compensation?

ParlaMint-IT_2015-07-30-LEG17-Senato-sed-494.ana.u665	CIOFFI (M5S). Mr President, it seems to me that this is the last amendment and, therefore, we are happy with one final explanation of vote. This is something we tried to explain earlier, when we were talking about sub-amendments, trying to counter the rule because we think it is incorrect. You give someone something, while there is someone else who takes something: it is the classic example of partition that the government wanted to do. We have allowed what once was the majority in Parliament to elect the board of directors with the current law, but preserving the characteristics of the managing director, which will be carried out by the general manager. We have tried to say that we do not believe this type of partition to be truly correct and fair. You can't understand why you have to pass a law and just take a piece of it. Maybe that little bit is the thing we're most interested in, having the famous superman who can do the good and the bad weather, maybe even the middle, with the superpowers we're giving him. The problem then, Mr President, is precisely this: we have already said a great deal that it could happen - we shall see it in the next few days - that becoming members of the Management Board are elected people in Parliament or in the regional councils - who knows - but in any case people who hold a political office. This is already in itself a vulnus, which we have tried to explain to the House, but which was obviously deaf to this request. And we did not really understand the reason for this deafness, but probably the names have already been distributed and perhaps one of them did not respond to certain characteristics. We do not understand how this can happen: we do not understand, that is, because we do not have the dignity to tell ourselves that we are stronger than the names that are reported to us. We didn't want to put that famous stake, which would have represented a vulnus of the new board of directors. I repeat, however, that we will see how things are going next week, provided that the new board of directors is voted on. Maybe it will be - as it were - a hit and go; let's see if we can do it right away, otherwise we will talk about it in September. We will see what will happen and what will be the ability to do this. However, if you want to elect the new RAI board with the Gasparri law, at least do not give it superpowers, otherwise we create a hybrid - we have already talked about it - a sort of genetically modified organism, although I seem to remember that the precautionary principle still exists in Europe compared to genetically modified organisms. And, in order to respect this principle, perhaps it was not appropriate to give the Director-General the powers of the CEO, because in this way we have built a somewhat strange hybrid, which unfortunately we do not like. We have never liked GMOs, let alone if we like a GMO with a human form. We dare not think of the form of this organism: perhaps the new director-general with superpowers will sprout four arms and maybe two heads; Maybe it'll be bicephalus, so we'll find ourselves in a rather strange situation, Mr. President, where we'll have...
